MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
March 7, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and MED-ENG, LLC, 
for the purchase of two (2) Bomb Suits for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police 
Department 
______________________________________________ 
This is a single source purchase through MED-ENG, LLC, therefore no bids were requested.  The 
MED-ENG, LLC Sole Manufacturer Statement is included for reference. 
The City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, was awarded a grant for the purchase of two 
(2) Bomb Suits, from a U.S. Department of Homeland Security Grant.   
The bomb suit requested by the Cheyenne Police Department is a Sole Source product, 
manufactured and sold exclusively through MED-ENG, LLC. 
The safety and functionality of the bomb suit is critical to the safety of the end user, and there are 
currently only two (2) bomb suits on the open market that are compliant with the NIJ 0117.01 
Public Safety Bomb Suit Standard, Safety Certification (Certified Products List from Safety 
Equipment Institute, An Affiliate Of ASTM International) is included for reference. 
The Cheyenne Police Department conducted research of both National Institute of Justice (NIJ) 
0117.01 compliant bomb suits.  Sergeant Zack Johnson (Cheyenne PD), along with Cheyenne 
Police Department Bomb Squad, conducted a review of the product offering from NP Aerospace, 
and concluded that there are several functions on the bomb suit that are not comparable to what 
MED-ENG, LLC is able to offer. For example, the bomb suit visor on the NP Aerospace helmet 
is very small, and does not appear to offer near the field of view that the MED-ENG, LLC EOD 
10E Helmet offers.  User visibility is critical while the suit is in use. 
As this procurement is conducted via a Federal Grant, MED-ENG, LLC’s active SAM.gov 
registration is included for reference.  Additionally, pricing research was conducted via GSA 
Advantage and buying the product directly from MED-ENG, LLC is less expensive, than if 
purchased via a GSA Cooperative Contract. 
 

The MED-ENG, LLC product has been recommended for approval by the City of Cheyenne Police 
Department, and is the basis for this request for Sole Source purchase.  The total requested amount 
is for $71,318.00 for the purchase of two (2) bomb suits, in accordance with the included vendor 
price quotation. 
 
Funding: 
025-21-2111-41-46528 (Federal Homeland Security Grant), Project #220020

STATE REASON FOR SOLE OR SINGLE SOURCE PURCHASE (CIRCLE ONE): 
After a thorough review of available bomb suits, Bomb Technicians on the team all agreed that Med-Eng EOD 10 
suits are the only one that meet our requirements and capabilities. Specifically, it is the only suit that meets standards for; 
fragmentation foot protection, helmet audio and hearing protection, drag rescue system, and 
electromagnetic interference and electromagnetic compatibility.

REQUEST TO PURCHASE OVER $35,000.00 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and MED-ENG, LLC, for the purchase 
of two (2) Bomb Suits for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, in an amount of 
$71,318.00.  This is a single source purchase, therefore no bids were requested.   
 
This will be reviewed by the City Council Finance Committee on March 21, 2022 and voted on 
at the March 28, 2022 City Council meeting.

Re: Sole Manufacturer Statement – Med-Eng Protective Equipment 
 
This letter confirms that Med-Eng Holdings ULC, and Med-Eng, LLC (“Med-Eng”), is the sole 
manufacturer of Med-Eng branded personal protective equipment (PPE) for Explosive 
Ordnance Disposal (EOD) and blast related operations. This equipment includes but is not 
limited to the EOD 10E Bomb Suit and Helmet ensemble.  
 
All manufacturing is conducted at our production facilities in Ogdensburg, New York, USA and 
in Pembroke, Ontario, Canada, or at a sub-contracted facility solely on behalf of Med-Eng.  
 
The EOD 10E is distinguished by the following set of specifications for the protection, safety, 
operability, and stress mitigation of the EOD operator. 
 
NIJ 0117.01 Certification - The EOD 10E is “Certified” to the NIJ 0117.01 Public Safety Bomb 
Suit Standard. Proof of certification includes being listed on the public website of the Safety 
Equipment Institute (SEI) certification body www.seinet.org. 
Note: SEI is the only organization that can confirm certification. Self-certification is not possible. 
A manufacturer or vendor claiming their EOD suit and or helmet “is compliant”, “meets”, 
“passes” or “is tested to” the NIJ 0117.01 Standard in the absence of official certification are not 
acceptable substitutes for proof of certification. 
Additional criteria that exceed the minimum requirements of the NIJ 0117.01 Standard are listed 
below and will serve to enhance the EOD technician’s blast protection, mission safety, fitment 
and operational efficiency. 
Electromagnetic Interference and Electromagnetic Compatibility – The EOD 10E Suit and 
Helmet ensemble, in its base configuration, complies with the MIL-STD-461F and the Def-Stan 
59-411 Class A Standards as listed in the following table, to limit both radiated electromagnetic 
emissions and susceptibility to electromagnetic radiation, as proven by documentation from an 
accredited NVLAP test facility.  
EMC

Helmet Audio and Hearing Protection – The EOD 10E Helmet includes stereophonic 
(left/right) integrated speakers built into the Helmet, with individually adjustable audio levels. 
The EOD 10E Helmet includes integrated protection against blast overpressure and audio noise 
in excess of 90 dBA. The noise level cut off threshold is adjustable by the user. 
Enhanced Fragmentation Face Shield Protection – The EOD 10E Helmet face shield is 
compatible with an optional steel visor to enhance V50 fragmentation protection at the face 
level, beyond the minimum NIJ 0117.01 V50 requirements. 
Fragmentation Foot Protection – The EOD 10E Suit includes 360-degree fragmentation 
protection to the foot and fits on American foot sizes of 6 to 14. The V50 level fragmentation 
protection is at least 450 m/s (1,475 feet/second) against the 17-grain (1.1 gram) Fragment 
Simulating Projectile (FSP). Documentation of this testing was conducted in accordance with 
the NIJ 0117.01 Public Safety Bomb Suit Standard.  
Slip Resistance - The EOD 10E foot protection has slip resistance independently proven to 
provide a static coefficient of friction of 0.60 or greater, as tested in accordance with ASTM F 
489, Standard Test Method for Static Coefficient of Friction of Shoe Sole and Heel Materials as 
measured by the James Machine, for dry conditions.  
Personal Cooling System – As documented by an independent test facility, when tested in 
accordance with ASTM F2371-05 (sweating thermal manikin testing), the EOD 10E ensemble 
provides 70 W of integrated cooling over the torso for a minimum of 120 minutes on a single 
battery serving as its primary power supply, without adding any additional weight to the suit. In 
addition, the EOD 10E ensemble is compatible with an optional body cooling system providing 
at least 145 W of average cooling to the body for a duration of at least 60 minutes. 
Warning Lights – The EOD 10E Helmet includes an LED mounted on the inside of the visor to 
notify and warn the operator of low battery level, connection problems, and other information in 
a manner not visible externally. 
Voice Command System – The EOD 10E Helmet includes an integrated Voice Command 
System that is capable of operating in multiple languages, including American English, to control 
helmet and suit functions, including ventilation, lighting (on/off, color choice, and white light 
intensity), headphone volume, and external speaker (on/off and volume).  The Voice Command 
System is interactive to facilitate usage. 
Drag Rescue System – The EOD 10E Suit jacket includes an enhanced integrated drag rescue 
system, in addition to the NIJ minimum requirements of shoulder handles, with a central 
attachment point aligned with the spine to grasp a fallen EOD operator and drag them in a 
controlled manner to a safe location for medical assistance.

Inflatable Helmet Liner – The EOD 10E Helmet includes an integrated inflatable helmet liner to 
fit the 5th percentile female to the 95th percentile male head sizes, as per the 2012 US Army 
anthropometric data. 
Helmet Lighting – The EOD 10E Helmet includes integrated LED lighting in white, red and 
blue, powered by the suit’s main power supply. Its White lights are adjustable in intensity and 
direction. The integrated lights remove the need for the operator to hold a flashlight, thereby 
freeing up the hands of the operator. Turning lights on and off, by color, is controlled by voice, to 
allow the operator to keep their hands free for other tasks.  
Control Unit Capabilities – To facilitate the work and concentration of the EOD technician, the 
EOD 10E Control Unit provides the following functional capabilities: 
• 
Display information for battery level, audio volume and fan speed  
• 
Control audio volume 
• 
Control fan speed 
• 
Countdown Timer 
• 
Stopwatch 
• 
Display fault diagnosis to assist in electronics system maintenance  
• 
Assign up to 4 pre-set user or environmental/mission profiles, such as stereophonic 
left/right speaker levels, to the electronics system to serve multiple team members who 
share a helmet 
Standard Components 
The EOD 10E ensemble includes the following components: 
• 
Jacket with integrated ventilation, back protector and detachable chest/groin plate; 
• 
Trousers with integrated yoke for trouser suspenders and an integrated waist 
cummerbund 
• 
Foot Protection 
• 
Helmet with visor, inflatable liner, integrated stereophonic speakers, microphone, and 
video camera mounting system

The total would be $75,357.92 if that cost was 
included in this subtotal.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM:

DATE: 
 
March 16, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Fire Rescue 
Department, and Stryker, for the purchase of a three (3) year maintenance 
agreement 
______________________________________________ 
This is a single source purchase through Stryker, therefore no bids were requested.  The Sole 
Source Letter is included for reference. 
This purchase is for a three (3) year maintenance agreement for existing Multi-Parameter 
Defibrillator Monitors, Chest Compression Devices, and Automated External Defibrillators, in an 
amount of $59,388.75.  The Stryker maintenance agreement is included for reference. 
The cost shall be divided equally into payments of $19,796.25, per year, for the three (3) year 
terms of the maintenance agreement. 
 
Funding: 
Cheyenne Fire Rescue – General Fund 

3/21/2022
3/28/2022
By TJ Barttelbort at 7:33 am, Mar 16, 2022

REQUEST TO PURCHASE OVER $35,000.00 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department, 
and Stryker, for the purchase of a three (3) year maintenance agreement for existing Multi-
Parameter Defibrillator Monitors, Chest Compression Devices, and Automated External 
Defibrillators, in an amount of $59,388.75.  This is a sole source purchase, therefore no bids were 
requested.   
 
This will be reviewed by the City Council Finance Committee on March 21, 2022 and voted on 
at the March 28, 2022 City Council meeting.

Pine Bluffs Gravel & Excavating, for the Yard Waste Material Hauling and Fill 
Dirt Hauling, for the City of Cheyenne, Compost Facility 
______________________________________________ 
 
Six (6) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Six (6) bids were received from: 
 
1. Pine Bluffs Gravel & Excavating.  Pine Bluffs, Wyoming. 
2. Earth Work Solutions (WY).  Gillette, Wyoming. 
3. RAM Trucking, Inc.  Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
4. TC Solutions.  Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
5. Rocky Mountain Sand & Gravel.  Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
6. JDub Hauling. Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Matt Theriault (Landfill / Compost), Craig LaVoy (Public Works), and 
Vicki Nemecek (Public Works), and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid 
from Pine Bluffs Gravel & Excavating, for a total bid award amount of $144,800.00.

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. E-8-22 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, _____, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and PINE 
BLUFFS GRAVEL & EXCAVATING, INC., hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Yard Waste Material Hauling and Fill Dirt Hauling” project 
all in strict accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and 
dated: March 4, 2022. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by April 15, 2023.  If the work has not been completed 
within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable 
delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as 
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is 
completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of One 
Hundred Forty-Four Thousand, Eight Hundred Dollars ($144,800.00).  In the event there are 
changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the 
actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The City 
agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon receipt of 
appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated work completed.  
Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due date as established 
annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be submitted on the Contract 
Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  
The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to payment. 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 9:55 am, Mar 23, 2022

ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation To Bid; 
4. General Provisions; 
 
5. Liquidated Damages; 
6. Specifications and Scope of Work; 
7. Signed Bid Proposal; and  
8. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said other 
documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms the 
Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has authorized 
the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the parties 
hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-08_TotalExtraction_45817202.pdf
 
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
March 23, 2022  
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-19-22 for furnishing 95 Gallon Automated Refuse Carts 
for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division  
______________________________________________ 
Three (3) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Three (3) bids were 
received from: 
 
1. Cascade Engineering, Inc.  Grand Rapids, Michigan. 
a. Multiple Bid Exceptions, Bid Rejected 
i. Page 9, Item 2, Composite Catch Bar, instead of Metal Catch Bar. 
ii. Page 9, Item 4, a, I, Height 46”, instead of maximum of 44”. 
iii. Page 10, Item 12, Non-Acceptance of City’s Mandatory Warranty 
Requirements. 
iv. Page 10, Item 15, a, b, c, d, e, f, In Mold Label, instead of Hot Stamped 
Instructions for Use. 
v. Page 12, Item 23, Non-Acceptance of City’s Mandatory Warranty 
Requirements. 
vi. Page 12, Item 24, Non-Acceptance of City’s Mandatory Warranty 
Requirements. 
vii. General, Material Adjustment Clause, the City did not request or provide 
provisions to utilize a Material Adjustment Clause. 
2. Otto Environmental Systems.  Charlotte, North Carolina. (Bid Options 1 & 2) 
a. Submitted Two Bid Options: 
i. Option 1 is fully compliant, Lowest Responsive Bid. 
ii. Option 2 contains Bid Exceptions. 
3. Able Equipment.  Casper, Wyoming. 
a. Bid Exceptions, Not Lowest Responsive Bidder. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Vicki Nemecek (Public Works), and Kaitlyn Garey (Sanitation).  Their 
recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Otto Environmental Systems, Bid 
Option 1, for the purchase of 95 Gallon Automated Refuse Carts, for a total cost of $38,539.20 for 
One (1) 53’ Semi-Trailer Load of Carts (560 Carts).

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-08_TotalExtraction_29793772.pdf
      
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 28, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave., and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, and Tom Segrave.  Absent were: 
Pete Laybourn and Jeff White. Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 14, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
      
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Prime Realty LLC d/b/a Prime Realty, 1601 
Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Dillinger’s, LLC d/b/a 
Dillinger’s, 1601 Central Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming).  There being no comments or input and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the 
application to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Wyoming Horse Racing LLC d/b/a Horse Palace, 
1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license held by Prime Realty LLC d/b/a Prime Realty, 
1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming). There being no comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the application 
to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a restaurant liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Osterfoss Living Trust Dated May 20, 2020 d/b/a Nagel-Warren 
Mansion, LLC, 222 E. 17th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Jas Barbe, Inn Keeper for Nagel-Warren 
Mansion, reported the mansion anticipates reopening in approximately 2 weeks.  Mr. Barbe 
requested consideration for approval and provided history of his Wyoming residency and 
commented on the historic value of the mansion and his excitement in being a part of its continued 
service to the community.  Supportive comments were voiced regarding the reopening and general 
support for downtown businesses.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the 

hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the application to 
Finance Committee. 
  
  
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint.  Reporting for 
Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by 
Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to postpone to April 11, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was 
noted that staff is working on the amendment language which is anticipated to be complete in the 
next 2 weeks.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.   
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
repealing UDC 5.4.7, UU – Urban Use Overlay District, and creating UDC 5.6.9, UU – Urban Use 
Overlay District.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4423) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from P Public District to CB Community Business 
for land located southwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Cleveland Avenue and U.S. 30.  
Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4424) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business for land located southeast of the intersection of College Drive and Charles 
Street.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. 
Voting “yes”- all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 9.01.010, Definitions of Title 9, 
Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. Johnson moved to postpone to April 
11, 2022, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Bob Wilson voiced support for the proposed postponement, 
and noted he is working with city staff on language amendments and expressed appreciation to 
Stefanie Boster, City Attorney, and council members Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson for the attention 
to his concerns.  Annette Williams expressed concern regarding the lack of support she feels she 
received at the March 22, 2022, Public Services Committee meeting.  Ms. Williams explained she 
had requested to speak following completion of action taken on the committee agenda and was 
told it was not customary for council committee meetings to receive public comment except for 
comment relating to items on the agenda in proper order and she questioned this practice.  It was 
noted that Public Services Committee Chairman Bryan Cook did allow Ms. Williams to speak as 
a courtesy.  Mayor Collins explained that the governing body allows for public comment on 
general city business at the end of every City Council meeting and that they are not required to 
and have not allowed for public comment periods at standing committees for many years.  Georgia 
Broyles, Youth Alternatives, questioned if there would be a process for School Resource Officers 
to ticket students violating the ordinance.  It was noted the request would be looked into. Sam 

Kayne and Pastor Tom Perea expressed support of the proposed postponement.  Motion to 
postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Repealing Ordinance No. 3465, and reestablishing and 
redefining ward boundaries within the City of Cheyenne -- referred to Public Services Committee. 
(SPONSOR – MR. COOK)  
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule -- referred to Finance Committee.  (SPONSOR – MR. WHITE)   
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Increasing the compensation for the Mayor of the City 
of Cheyenne, commencing January 1, 2025 -- referred to Finance Committee.  (SPONSOR – MR. 
ROYBAL)    
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Increasing the compensation for members of the City 
Council of the City of Cheyenne and providing an effective date -- referred to Finance Committee. 
(SPONSOR – MR. ROYBAL)   
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Uncommitting $329,630.00 in General Fund Reserves approved to be 
used for the purchase of the former Hitching Post, Lot 1, Block 1, P.A.S. Subdivision by the City 
of Cheyenne Governing Body on July 22, 2019. Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6219) 
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Olivet 
Cemetery, 4th Filing, a replat of a portion of Olivet Cemetery, 3rd Filing and plat of a portion of 
the southeast quarter of the southeast quarter of Section 30, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, 
of the 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of 
Pershing Boulevard and Airport Parkway).  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave. 
Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute maps containing technical corrections, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion to amend by substitute maps carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#6220) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Rocking 
A at Sweetgrass, a subdivision of a replat of all of Lots 100 & 200, Block 15, Inclusive, Sweetgrass, 
6th Filing and all of Tract U Sweetgrass, 2nd Filing along with a + 61,911 sqft portion of abutting 
unplatted lands situated in the N1/4 of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th 
P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located South of East Murray Road East and 
adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive). Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6221)  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – To authorize the application for the Suicide Intervention Grant in the 
amount of $73,582.00 from Cheyenne Regional Medical Center for the City of Cheyenne, Office 
of Youth Alternatives.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6222)  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Approving water and sanitary sewer services, by outside user water and 
sanitary sewer services agreements, for Dean Byrne, for Lot 3, Energy Park One, situated in the 

NE¼ of Section 33, Township 13 North, Range 67 West, of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, 
Wyoming. Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6223)  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Wyoming 
Department of Transportation, Highway Safety Program, Grant Funding for the City’s mobile 
D.U.I. Command Vehicle (Highway Safety Behavioral Grant) – referred to Finance Committee.  
(SPONSOR – DR. ALDRICH) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – For Governing Body support of Community forum(s) and other 
appropriate initiatives to encourage more kindness, civility, cooperation, and respectful dialogue 
– referred to Public Services Committee.  (SPONSORS – DR. ALDRICH AND MR. 
LAYBOURN) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Directing City staff to repair the tree lawn area on Vandehei Avenue 
between Yellowstone Road and Stockman Street. (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax fund and General 
Fund Reserves) – referred to Public Services Committee.  (SPONSOR – MR. SEGRAVE) 
 
 
Agreement between the Board of Public Utilities and Polo Ranch Company regarding 
Quarter Circle Five subdivision well development and associated water entitlement.  Hank Bailey 
advised he is on the Polo Ranch Company Board of Directors and has proposed language to satisfy 
legal terms of the agreement.  Mr. Bailey provided information relating to past and proposed water 
usage involving rural properties and future development of residential homes.  Levi Martin, area 
resident, voiced concerns involving the city relinquishing water rights, fairness and previously 
agreed upon policy terms.  Brad Brooks, BOPU Director, upon inquiry provided information 
involving a previous water usage study completed in Laramie County.  Mr. Brooks provided 
information relating to proposed water usage allowances and actual usage on the Polo Ranch and 
requirement of the ranch to pay for any overuse, noting the Polo Ranch water usage is monitored.  
Mr. Brooks reported a previous Supreme Court opinion that determined a use of .7 acre feet per 
minute for county residential properties and advised he feels that is a fair assessment.  It was noted 
that the water use of the proposed residential home development would not be metered.  Comment 
was made that the state allows for up to 25 acre feet per minute for rural wells.  Mr. Brooks further 
noted that the proposed assessment provides a savings to the aquafer over all, noting support of 
the stipulations in this agreement. Information was provided regarding the 1955 agreement.  Mr. 
Johnson moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Johnson moved to amend by adding 
#10 which states “the Polo Ranch Company hereby acknowledges the binding rulings of the 
Wyoming Supreme Court in their 1998 and 2003 opinions in litigation involving Polo Ranch 
Company and the  City of Cheyenne and hereby expressly agrees that the 1955 Drilling and Water 
Use Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and John and Marie Bell has been finally and fully 
adjudicated and that the Polo Ranch has no claims or causes of action against the City of Cheyenne 
based on that Agreement or its implementation.”, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.     Main motion as amended 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook 
and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  (#7487) 
 

[CA] 
Contract between the City of Cheyenne, the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities, 
and McGee, Hearne & Paiz LLP for financial and compliance auditing services for fiscal the year 
ending June 30, 2022 (General Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7488) 
 
 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Umpire’s Association to 
umpire and score the Recreation Division Adult Softball Leagues and Tournaments for 2022 
(Recreation Programs Fund).   Mr. Johnson moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Mr.

[CA] 
2022-2023 Collective Labor Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne

Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County School 
District Number One to outline responsibilities for public infrastructure improvements relating to 
the Coyote Ridge Elementary School Project (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax).  Mr.  Johnson moved to 
approve in an amount not to exceed $177,000, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Planning & Development 
Director Charles Bloom advised the agreement allows for the city to contribute to funding for 
improvements on Carlson Road, a land swap for land located near South High School and 
Harmony Valley.  Mr. Bloom reported the city can ask for improvement reimbursement when 
adjacent land is developed in the future and that when the land is developed, the developers will 
be required to pay the city for the road.  It was noted the city will recoup approximately $76,000 
for the land swap.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  (#7491)

Appointments for Erin LeBlanc to the Board of Adjustment and Elizabeth Bales to the 
Historic Preservation Board.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mayor 
Collins upon inquiry provided information on the application and selection process for mayoral 
appointments to boards and commissions.  Mayor Collins noted that it is possible for individuals 
to serve on multiple community boards at the same time and that the application by Elizabeth Bales 
was not vetted by the Historic Preservation Board prior to his recommendation to appoint Ms. 
Bales to the board.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (Mayoral appointment). 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 11, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, to hear public comment regarding the City’s intent to purchase property located at 1800 
Westland Road, Cheyenne, Wyoming for the purpose of relocating the Cheyenne Transit System 
Office and Transfer Station. 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 11, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for one available retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office by 
Ace’s Range LLC d/b/a Ace’s Range LLC, 1505 Pioneer Ave., Big D Oil Co. d/b/a Big D Oil #56, 
4100 Whitney Rd., Billy Jacks - Wyoming LLC d/b/a Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub, 4020 Airport 
Parkway West, Suite 1, Capitol Cuisine LLC d/b/a Ike’s Bar & Grill, 122 W. 6th St., Ful-O-Pep 
d/b/a The Old Cheyenne Elevator, 800 W. 21st St., Get Bent, LLC d/b/a Get Bent, LLC, 707 W. 
Lincolnway, Maurice W. Brown d/b/a Town & Country Supermarket Liquors-North, 2120 Carey 
Ave., Space 1, Peaches’ Package Liquor & Bar LLC d/b/a Peaches’ Package Liquor & Bar LLC, 
Anderson Corner, 3rd Filing, Lot 2, Block 1, Vitamin College Natural Food Markets, Inc. d/b/a 
Natural Grocers, 1851 Dell Range Blvd., Westby Edge LLC d/b/a Westby Edge LLC, 714 W. 20th 
St. and Wyoming Downs OTB 14 LLC d/b/a Wyoming Downs OTB 14, 1345 Dell Range Blvd., 
Cheyenne. 
 
 
Under other business:   
 
 
Annette Williams advised that she felt that she did not get the same treatment at the March 
22, 2022 Public Services Committee as other members of the public and felt disrespected.  Ms. 
Williams requested approval to hang military memorial banners in downtown and other areas of 
town during the Veteran’s Day holiday.  She noted that family members of fallen military would 
be paying for the banners and the brackets will be used if approved by the Board of Public Utilities.  
Ms. Williams inquired about amending the current UDC and City Code sign regulations to allow 
for banners to be hung during certain time frames.  Mayor Collins advised that unfortunately by 
allowing one group to hang banners it opens up the possibility that the city would be required to 
allow any group to hang banners.  Mayor Collins advised groups such as “hate groups” that may 
offend others would be allowed to hang their banners and reported that other Wyoming towns have 
been sued relating to this issue.  Mayor Collins reported that he is looking into options to allow 

groups such as the military to be able to be honored in ways suggested by Ms. Williams.  Ms. 
Williams inquired about scooters randomly placed around town and if that was a code violation.  
City Attorney Stefanie Boster advised they are not in violation of right of way ordinances.    
 
 
Jim Ridgeway commented on revisions made to recent bid inquiries involving city surplus 
firearms.  He voiced concerns relating to the city’s ability to sell firearms without serial numbers, 
absence of visual inspection either in person or by photographs and method of current storage by 
the city.    
 
 
Mayor Collins upon inquiry advised the Municipal Building repairs are near completion 
and that there does not appear there were financial savings during staff’s absence from the building 
during the past 6 months.     
 
 
 Bob Wilson voiced support for the postponement on the ordinance relating to creating a 
new Section 9.01.010-Definitions of Title 9 Public Peace and Welfare and looking into code 
changes relating to community signage.    
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:15 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
April 13, 2022 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-28_TotalExtraction_88146267.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 14, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave., and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Ken Esquibel 
(via Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal and Jeff White.  
Absent: Mr. Cook and Mr. Segrave.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
 
PROCLAMATION – Designating February 12-19, 2022 as Entrepreneurship Week.  
Mayor Collins read the proclamation into the record and expressed appreciation to members of the 
Innovation and Entrepreneur Advisory Council who were in attendance. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Johnson and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item #25(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. Roybal, 
agenda item #17 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Johnson moved to approve 
remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 24, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for 521 Southwest Drive (located east of 
Southwest Drive, south of I-80).  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, provided 
information on the property and reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation conditions 
contained in state statutes.  Mr. Lloyd noted the affiliated annexation and zone change ordinances 
are also on the agenda.  Upon request, Mr. Lloyd provided examples of developments for the 
Mixed-Use Employment zone category.  There being no further comments or input and purpose 
of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the Zoning Classification from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use 
Business Emphasis for land located northwest of the intersection of Nationway and Logan Avenue.  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.  (#4415) 

 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 13.04 – Public Utilities Generally; 
Chapter 13.08 – Water Service System; Chapter 13.12 – Sewer System; Chapter 13.16 – Storm 
Sewers; and Chapter 13.20 – Wastewater Treatment & Disposal; of Title 13 – Public Services, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved 
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to Table 5-1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Districts 
and Uses, amending the Permissibility of Various Uses – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint – referred to Public 
Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80 – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor 
– Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use to AG Agricultural for 
land located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80 – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to HI Heavy 
Industrial for land located north of Campstool Road, west of Progress Circle – referred to Public 
Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Creating a new Section 9.08.120, Malicious Harassment 
Based on Race, Color, Religion, Ancestry, Sex, Sexual Orientation, Gender Identity and 
Expression, National Origin, or Disability, of Chapter 9.08, Offenses Against Public Peace and 
Decency, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – 
referred to Committee of the Whole. (Sponsors – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, 
Mr. Roybal and Mr. White) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 12.20, Cemeteries, 
of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne pertaining 
to Cemetery fees – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Segrave and Mr. White) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing up to a $14,890,000 interfund loan from the One Percent 
Sales Tax Capital Projects Fund to the Special Purpose Option Sales Tax Capital Projects Fund to 
facilitate the construction of three fire stations, purchase fire trucks and replace the cemetery 
irrigation system as approved by voters on the November 2, 2021 Sixth Penny Ballot.  Jim 

Ridgeway advised he is in support of the project and inquired as to when construction would take 
place.  John Kopper, Chief of the Fire Department, advised the city has a Professional Services 
Agreement with Plan One Architects and the projects are almost shovel ready, estimating the first 
fire station to be ready for construction by June.  Upon inquiry, Chief Kopper provided the 
following information: the public areas of the fire stations will meet ADA requirements, each staff 
member on duty will have their own bedroom in the new fire stations and there will be 3 separate 
bathrooms.  County Commissioner Gunner Malm expressed appreciation to Mayor Collins and 
the governing body for looking at this funding option, noting it is a great opportunity to save 
taxpayers money on the projects and to everyone who provided information to the public on the 
6th Penny projects as they are instrumental in the community.  Mayor Collins advised that due to 
inflation, the cost will continue to rise for construction of the fire stations and this approach 
provides for financial responsibility and accountability to the voters.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Concerns and comments were made relating to the Consent Agenda 
process regarding public comment options.  City Treasurer Robin Lockman, upon inquiry, advised 
this type of funding option was utilized in the past for construction of the David R. Romero Park.  
Ms. Lockman further advised the funds come out of 5th Penny fund, provided information on 
available funding in the 5th Penny fund and noted it will not affect reserves.  Mayor Collins 
explained utilizing this funding option includes repayment to the fund with interest.  Appreciation 
was expressed to Ms. Lockman and her team for their outstanding services and their assistance 
with the resolution.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
(#6207) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Emergency Management Agency 
Preparedness Grant under the Staffing for Adequate Fire and Emergency Response (SAFER) 
Program, on behalf of the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, for the purpose of adding nine 
new firefighter positions.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6208) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Certifying Planning Commission Action amending the Future Land Use 
Maps of PlanCheyenne changing the land use category from Community Business to Mixed-Use 
Commercial for property generally located west of Campbell Avenue, north of Nationway, and 
south of and adjacent to E. Lincolnway.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6209) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Adopting the revised City of Cheyenne, Wyoming Investment Policy 
Statement – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor- Mr. White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to approve and file the Wyoming 
Division of Victim Services Funding Application State Fiscal Year 2023 and 2024, July 1, 2022 
to June 30, 2024 – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White)  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Sweetgrass, 9th Filing, a Replat of all of Lot 1, Block 4, Sweetgrass, 1st Filing situated in the N1/2 
of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located south of East Murray Drive west and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive) – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook)  
 

[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Adopting a fee schedule for Planning and Development projects – 
referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White)  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – For Governing Body approval of a one-year Bronze Boot Sponsor 
relationship between Cheyenne Frontier Days and Miller/Coors for 2022 – referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White)  
 
 
Grant of Right-of-Way Easement on City Owned Property, per Exhibit A, between the City 
of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power Company for a 150’ wide right-of-way and 
easement for construction and maintenance of overhead power facilities.  Jim Wasson, Cheyenne 
Light Fuel and Power Company, advised he was available for any questions.  Charles Bloom, 
Director of Planning and Development, requested the item be postponed to allow for research on 
the $10 amount affiliated with the easement.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Mr. White moved to postpone to February 28, 2022, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. 
Wasson explained the $10 fee is just a placeholder as compensation must be put towards the 
agreement. He further noted landowners in the vicinity pay $15/linear foot.  Motion to postpone 
carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) between City of Cheyenne and Doran Lummis to 
authorize filing of platting applications(s) for City-owned lands for the purpose of access to future 
development.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7472) 
 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Thomas Szott for City 
Prosecutor legal counsel services (General Fund).  Mr. White advised the item was reviewed under 
other items for the agenda at the February 7, 2022 Finance Committee meeting, therefore, 
provisions of Title 2 of City Code have been met.  Mr. White moved to adopt in an amount not to 
exceed $86,400.00 annually, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised Mr. 
Szott will serve as the City Prosecutor and expressed the need to fill the position is immediate.   
City Attorney Stefanie Boster explained, upon inquiry, that the intent is for Mr. Szott to serve in a 
part time capacity to allow him time to work on his private practice, noting currently due to the 
demand of city cases, he is currently working full time.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (#7473) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Kyle M. Brandon for 
City Public Defender legal counsel services (General Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Veolia ES Technical 
Solutions, LLC, for the Hazardous Waste Disposal Services Project (Solid Waste Fund) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Jones Land 
Surveying, Inc., for professional Land Surveying Services (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 

[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Steil Surveying 
Services, LLC, for professional Land Surveying Services (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to 
Finance Committee.   
 
 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6957 between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming 
Cattle Co., LLC adding the Mariah Pasture and extending the term of the Belvoir Ranch Grazing 
Lease Agreement through December 31, 2024 (Revenue to the City).  Mr. White advised the 
recommendation of the Finance Committee was to approve in an amount not to exceed 
$187,477.50, however, it has been brought to his attention that due to the nature of the contract, 
the amount of revenue may fluctuate annually depending on the condition of the grass and the 
amount of grazing.  Mr. White moved to approve in the amount of $187,477.50, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Upon inquiry, Craig LaVoy, Deputy Director of Public Works, explained the Mariah 
Pasture is no longer considered over-grazed and the Conservation District supports continuation 
of grazing.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#6957) 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #3 to Contract #6441 between the City of Cheyenne and Microsoft 
Corporation for the renewal to the City’s Enterprise Office 365 Online Enrollment.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6441) 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7118 for the Memorandum of Understanding 
between the City of Cheyenne and Saddle Ridge, LLC, to outline the responsibilities for the 
donation of land and for the management of a multi-purpose non-motorized pedestrian and bicycle 
pathway, park and landscape amenities in a portion of Saddle Ridge, 7th and 9th Filings 
(Community Facility Fees) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7119 for the Memorandum of Understanding 
between the City of Cheyenne and WJE, LLC, to outline the responsibilities for the donation of 
land and for the management of a multi-purpose non-motorized pedestrian and bicycle pathway, 
park and landscape amenities in a portion of Saddle Ridge, 8th, 9th, 10th, 11th, 12th and 13th Filings 
(Community Facility Fees) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-7-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and S&S 
Builders, LLC, for the Street and Alley Department Tent Building Repairs and Door Repairs 
Project (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid in an 
amount not to exceed $105,104.69). (#7474)  
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-8-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Front 
Range Roofing Systems, LLC, for the Ice and Events Center Roof Replacement Project (General 
Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid in an amount not to exceed 
$181,650.00). (#7475) 
 
[CA] 
Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and KONE, Inc., for the repair 
of the South East Hydraulic Elevator located at the Jack Spiker Parking Garage (General Fund 
Reserves).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $42,655.00). 
 

[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-11-22 for furnishing eight (8) light poles (17th Street Lighting), 
for the City of Cheyenne, Engineering Department (Specific Purpose Sales Tax) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
               
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
Announcement was made that due to the Presidents Day holiday on Monday, February 21, 
2022, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held as follows: 
Finance Committee will meet at Noon on Tuesday, February 22, 2022 and Public Services 
Committee will meet at Noon on Wednesday, February 23, 2022 at the Kiwanis Community House 
at 4603 Lions Park Drive, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting. 
 
Announcement was made of a Committee of the Whole meeting to be held Wednesday, 
February 23, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, 
Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting. 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, for renewal applications for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, 
limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, special malt beverage 
and winery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.    
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for Dillon Companies, LLC, a Kansas limited liability company d/b/a King 
Soopers, 3702 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Avanti, Inc., 
d/b/a Dell Range Liquor, 3806 Dell Range Blvd. #4, Cheyenne, Wyoming)  
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, to hear public comment regarding the City’s intent to enter into a contract 
with Comea Shelter to grant a subaward from Coronavirus State and Local Fiscal Recovery Funds 
to be used to increase available emergency housing and temporary shelter for Cheyenne’s 
homeless population. 
 
 
Under other business:   
 
 
Upon inquiry by Mayor Collins, City Clerk Kris Jones advised the following item was 
being introduced for the purpose of being referred to Finance Committee to be held on February 
22, 2022 at the Kiwanis Community House:  Contract between the City of Cheyenne and the 
Comea Shelter granting a subaward to be used to increase available emergency housing and 
temporary shelter to support Cheyenne’s homeless population.  (Coronavirus State and Local 
Fiscal Recovery Funds) Mayor Collins referred the item to the Finance Committee.   
    
    
 

 
Jim Ridgeway expressed concern relating to residential sewer fees being billed for 35 day 
periods and how the extra days affect the budgeted bill amount.  Mr. Ridgeway advised the Board 
of Public Utilities explained the late readings were due to staffing issues within Zone 17.  Mr. 
Ridgeway noted he does not believe the consumer should have to pay for the inefficiencies.  Mayor 
Collins advised water and sewer rates are established during the winter months.  Mr. Ridgeway 
provided photos and information regarding Lions Park walking paths and advised there are issues 
with standing water and drainage problems which freezes and causes safety hazards.  Mr. 
Ridgeway inquired about the Bird Scooters and requirements of the company relating to picking 
up abandon scooters.  Mayor Collins advised users can leave them in areas around town and the 
company is responsible for retrieving and maintaining them.  Mr. Johnson provided detailed 
information regarding reporting concerns involving the scooters and advised residents can find 
additional information on the city’s and Bird Rides’ websites.  Mr. Johnson provided additional 
information relating to violations of city code involving the scooters.  Mr. Ridgeway advised he 
believes it is a burden on citizens to contact the company.  Compliance Director Eric Fountain, 
upon inquiry, reported an annual review will be held soon to address any issues involving the 
scooters.  Deputy Community Recreation and Events Director Jason Sanchez, upon inquiry, 
provided information relating to snow removal in Lions Parks and advised he would look into 
drainage concerns.  Mr. Ridgeway further referenced a memo provided by City Treasurer Robin 
Lockman on January 18th related to the resolution authorizing the interfund loan for various 
November 2, 2021 Sixth Penny Ballot projects and the funding of the 2019-2022 5th Penny ballot, 
noting overages collected that could be allotted for other needed improvements. 
 
 
Dr. Aldrich advised there will be a City Council Ward III Council and Coffee event on 
Saturday, February 19th, from 9 am - 11 am at Dazbog Coffee (3911 E. Pershing Blvd.).  
 
 
Mr. Johnson commended colleagues for their attendance and participation at the annual 
Boys & Girls Club Chili Cook and expressed appreciation to Triumph High Culinary Club for 
their 3rd Place win.  Mayor Collins advised the fundraising event included a friendly competition 
between members of the Governing Body and the County Commissioners.  Dr.  Aldrich reported 
the event raised almost $20k between the fundraising efforts of the Governing Body and the 
County Commissioners. 
 
Mr. Roybal moved to meet in executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16-4-
405(a)(x), seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present.  Mayor Collins advised the executive session will be held to discuss the upcoming 
annual Fire Union contract and announced members would meet in executive session, not open to 
the public; requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, City Treasurer, Chief of Staff and Fire Chief 
be in attendance and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of the 
executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the close 
of the executive session. (Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 7:02 p.m.).     
 
 
Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 7:58 p.m. 
 
 

 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:58 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
February 25, 2022

Members of City Council
FROM:

DATE:
February 23, 2022
RE:
City Liquor License Renewals for 2022-2023
Attached is a listing of those applicants applying for renewal of existing City liquor licenses
(i.e.: retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar & grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, 
special malt beverage and winery permits), liquor licensee actions that occurred during this past
licensing year, the Police Department’s compliance report and heat maps indicating locations of all
license locations.  Public hearing for the applications is scheduled during the February 28, 2022 City
Council meeting.
Due to the large volume of paperwork involved in renewing the liquor licenses, the attached
listing provides the “basics” from application information in a readable format.  If you would like
to review an individual application file, please contact me and I will make it available to you.
If you have any questions concerning applicant information or renewal requirements and
procedures, please do not hesitate to contact me at your convenience.

My commission expires:________________ 


 
 
February 7, 2022 
Mayor and City Council 
Re: 
Application for a transfer of ownership and expansion of location as to Dell Range 
Liquors’ retail license 
Dear Mr. Mayor and City Councilors: 
We have enclosed an application for a transfer of retail liquor license number 46 that has been issued to 
Avanti, Inc. dba Dell Range Liquor.  The proposed transfer is to Dillon Companies, LLC, which is the 
owner/operator of the King Soopers store to the west of Dell Range Liquors.  In addition, the application 
seeks to expand the liquor license area from the approximately 5,600 sqft site that currently exists to an 
approximately 7,278 sqft site.    
As is required under Cheyenne’s zoning code, any expansion of a liquor license conditional use permit 
requires approval of the Cheyenne Board of Adjustment.  On December 16, 2021, the Board of 
Adjustment approved the conditional use permit expansion of the liquor store here.  A site plan shows 
the boundaries of the liquor store and liquor storage area.    
Thus, we apply for the Mayor and Council’s consideration of a transfer of ownership and approval of the 
expanded store area.  We believe that Dillon Companies, LLC will be a suitable successor to Mr. Dimarzio 
and Mr. Mancini who have operated this liquor license since 2000 as Avanti, Inc.  King Soopers has been 
a fixture in this neighborhood for many years, and we greatly enjoy being a part of the Cheyenne 
community. 
The transaction between Avanti, Inc. and Dillon Companies, LLC is contingent on the City approving the 
liquor license transfer.  Upon an approval of the transfer, the parties can quickly close.  We respectfully 
request that any approval be conditioned on the closing of the transaction between Avanti, Inc. and 
Dillon Companies, LLC. 
We look forward to speaking to you in your consideration of this proposed transfer, and appreciate the 
time you invest into consideration of items like this. 
Very truly yours, 
 
 
Teresa Dietz

Proposed Alcohol Training Program 
pursuant to City Code Chapter 5.12 requirements 
 
The Cheyenne City Code imposes the following criteria, which must be met in order for a 
licensee to meet required review criteria.  The applicant proposes to complete all of the following 
requirements, which are set forth in City Code 5.12.090.  The applicant provides “TIPS” 
(Training for Intervention ProcedureS) type training at its locations that sell alcoholic beverages, 
and so applicant is familiar with providing training and in meeting compliance requirements in 
relation to liquor sales. 
 
Applicant shall:  
 
• Provide initial orientation training on alcohol and malt beverage related sales and service 
for new employees and all volunteers who will be selling or furnishing alcohol or malt 
beverages, and on-site managers and persons who may provide on-site security services 
on behalf of the licensee or permittee upon the premises. 
 
• Provide training prior to any duties being performed that relate to alcohol and malt 
beverage sales and service. Orientation training materials must be approved by the chief 
of police. 
 
• Ensure that persons who physically sell, serve or deliver alcoholic liquor or malt 
beverages at retail on or off the premises of a licensee or permittee, and on-site managers 
and any persons providing on-site security services successfully complete an alcohol 
training program as approved by the Wyoming Liquor Division pursuant to state law and 
the division's rules and regulations. Program participants must successfully complete the 
program within ninety (90) days of employment or initial date of providing services.  
 
• Maintain, upon the business premises, a current list of bona fide employees, volunteers, 
on-site managers and any persons providing on-site security services; date of hire/service; 
dates of orientation training and successful completion of the approved training program, 
including any required continuing training. The list shall be available for inspection by 
law enforcement personnel. 
 
• Prohibit sales to and consumption by persons under the legal drinking age within the 
licensed or permitted building or authorized areas of service. 
 
• Report to local law enforcement by the licensee, permittee, employee, volunteer, on-site 
manager, agents or any persons providing on-site security services, any incidents upon 
the premises requiring law enforcement response services. Immediate notification to the 
police department shall be provided when any criminal or illegal activity occurs or is 
observed in or upon the licensee's or permittee's property involving the licensed building 
and adjoining property affiliated with the sale or consumption of alcoholic liquor and 
malt beverages, including the reporting of any assault, aggravated assault, or assault and 
battery.
